### Pass 10 — open-box classification and verification scope

Baseline: **277/364 checked; 87 open; Master 95/97** at `5a22cf80`. Word, Markdown, mapping and current state reconcile; the existing audit reports `problems: []`. No checkbox changes are made by this classification.

At the initial Pass 10 checkpoint, another task was editing storefront, branding and Verified Suppliers, so that work was excluded from the initial verification. The owner later confirmed that only this chat is writing. Current batches use the isolated committed baseline and preserve all unrelated pending work through guarded integration; remaining screen audits must include the preserved storefront. Pending work does not count as external completion evidence.

A = software-verifiable; B = software plus outstanding external/human evidence; C = human/external evidence; D = mutually exclusive form choices; E = duplicate/dependent software checks. Sign-off choices additionally require named human decision-makers. These are work classifications, not completion claims.

Baseline classification totals: A: 27; B: 13; C: 7; D: 30; E: 10. Exact requirements, ordinals, classifications and remaining actions: `verification-evidence/pass10/remaining.json`.

## How to verify each row yourself

Start the stack with `.\scripts\dev-stack.ps1` (storefront http://localhost:5174, admin http://localhost:5173, API http://localhost:4000). Each row lists what to open and what you should see, then the automated tests that prove the same thing. Run backend tests from `backend` and storefront tests from `apps/customer-web`, one run at a time: they share the test database.

### Row 1 — Home / Landing (FIXED_AND_VERIFIED)

1. Open http://localhost:5174/ as a guest. Under the headline you should see one sentence: "Source direct from verified suppliers…". With suppliers in more than one country it names no country; if every approved seller is registered in one country it says "…in <country>".
2. Scroll below "Shop by category": a "Verified suppliers" section shows up to 8 cards, each with name, kind · country, "Verified since <Month Year>" (or "Verified by <marketplace>") and a product count.
3. Press a card: it opens /suppliers/<slug> (the supplier page, row 5).
4. In the admin (http://localhost:5173) suspend one of those sellers, reload the home page: that card is gone. Reinstate it afterwards.
5. Negative: open http://localhost:4000/api/v1/catalog/suppliers?limit=99 - you get 400. The JSON of ?limit=8 contains no legal names.
6. Switch the language (EN menu top right) to Polski or Deutsch: the section and sentence are translated; switch the theme to dark: the cards stay readable.

Automated checks:

```powershell
Set-Location backend; npx vitest run tests/integration/catalog-suppliers.test.ts
Set-Location apps/customer-web; npx vitest run src/components/home/VerifiedSuppliers.test.tsx
```

### Row 2 — Global Search Results (FIXED_AND_VERIFIED)

1. Search "fasten" from the home page: above the results, "Verified suppliers matching "fasten"" lists suppliers whose public name matches; each pill opens their supplier page.
2. Add a price filter (e.g. From 100): the chip reads "From ₹100.00"; switch to Deutsch - it reads "Ab ₹100.00".
3. Market eligibility (no admin screen yet - Master row 69): add a row to market_rules with effect BLOCK for one product and your chosen country (or run the automated test below). Choose that country in the EN/₹ menu: the product disappears from search and category listings; choose another country: it is back.
4. Negative: http://localhost:4000/api/v1/catalog/suppliers?q=<121 characters> returns 400.

Automated checks:

```powershell
Set-Location backend; npx vitest run tests/integration/catalog-market-eligibility.test.ts
Set-Location apps/customer-web; npx vitest run src/components/catalog/SupplierMatches.test.tsx
```

### Row 3 — Category / Industry Landing (FIXED_AND_VERIFIED)

1. Open http://localhost:5174/category/tools-hardware. Under "What is inside" a "Sourcing Tools & Hardware" panel shows "N verified suppliers sell in Tools & Hardware." with up to 6 supplier pills.
2. Press a pill: the category page opens filtered to that supplier (?seller=…), with a "Supplier: <name>" chip that removes the filter.
3. Press "Ask the assistant about sourcing Tools & Hardware" (only when the assistant is switched on): AI Mode opens with the question already typed in the composer, not sent.
4. With a BLOCK or DOCUMENTS_REQUIRED category rule for your chosen country (see row 2), the panel shows the operator's reason, and for documents the list of documents.

Automated checks:

```powershell
Set-Location backend; npx vitest run tests/integration/catalog-market-eligibility.test.ts
Set-Location apps/customer-web; npx vitest run src/components/catalog/CategorySourcing.test.tsx
```

### Row 4 — Product Detail Page (FIXED_AND_VERIFIED)

1. Open a product sold by a seller, e.g. http://localhost:5174/product/firstcry-toys-for-kids-t-001-s3ch85. Under "Ordering information" the block "Who sells it and how it reaches you" shows Sold by <seller> (a link), kind · country, "Verified since …".
2. Without a country chosen, Delivery says "Choose your country at the top of the page…". Choose India in the EN/₹ menu: it says "Can be sold and delivered to India."
3. Open a marketplace-owned product (e.g. the dehumidifier demo product): Sold by reads "<marketplace>, from its own stock" and there is no inspection line.
4. Suspension (defect fixed): in admin suspend the seller of a product; reload the product - the seller is no longer named and adding it to the cart binds another seller or is refused. Reinstate afterwards.
5. Accessibility: run any screen reader or axe DevTools on the product page - the Ordering information and sourcing lists report no definition-list errors.

Automated checks:

```powershell
Set-Location backend; npx vitest run tests/integration/catalog-product-sourcing.test.ts tests/integration/marketplace-catalogue.test.ts
Set-Location apps/customer-web; npx vitest run src/components/product/ProductSourcingPanel.test.tsx
```

### Row 5 — Supplier Storefront (FIXED_AND_VERIFIED)

1. Open http://localhost:5174/suppliers/northwind-fastenings: name, logo, kind · country, "Verified since …", "See its N products" and Website (opens in a new tab).
2. "About the supplier" and "What they sell here" appear; each category pill opens that category filtered to this supplier.
3. Certifications, Factories and Capabilities appear only when that data exists (entered from Master rows 13/34); only certificates the operator verified and still in date are listed; factories show city/region/country, never a street address.
4. Open http://localhost:5174/suppliers/jkl (a draft seller): the not-found page. The API http://localhost:4000/api/v1/catalog/suppliers/jkl returns 404, and the JSON for a real supplier contains no legal name, GSTIN or contact details.

Automated checks:

```powershell
Set-Location backend; npx vitest run tests/integration/catalog-supplier-profile.test.ts
Set-Location apps/customer-web; npx vitest run src/pages/SupplierPage.test.tsx
```

### Row 6 — Compare Products / Suppliers (FIXED_AND_VERIFIED)

1. Open two product pages and press "Compare" under each title; the button turns into "In comparison" and offers "Compare 2 items".
2. Open http://localhost:5174/compare: a table with the products as columns and Price, Minimum order, Ordered in, Sold by, Delivery, Prepared for dispatch in, Country of origin, Inspection and the specifications as rows.
3. Choose a country in the EN/₹ menu: the Delivery row changes for every product (values are read fresh, not remembered).
4. Press "Remove …" under a column; press "Clear this comparison": the empty state explains how to add items.
5. Open two supplier pages, press Compare, then the "Suppliers" view on /compare: kind, country, verified since, products, verified certifications, factories, capacity, exports, reply time.
6. Try to add a fifth product: "You can compare up to 4 at a time…" appears and nothing is added. On a phone width the table scrolls sideways inside its frame; the page itself does not.

Automated checks:

```powershell
Set-Location apps/customer-web; npx vitest run src/pages/ComparePage.test.tsx
```

### Row 7 — How Gloviaa Assurance Works (FIXED_AND_VERIFIED)

1. Open http://localhost:5174/assurance (also "How assurance works" in the footer, and at the foot of the "Who sells it…" block on any product page).
2. Check the six cards: the verified-supplier count equals the number on the home page section; the return window equals Admin → returns settings; the claim/seller/decision/appeal numbers equal Admin → dispute settings.
3. With no inspection rules configured, "Inspection before dispatch" says it is not required at the moment. Add an active inspection rule in the admin console and reload: it now explains that goods cannot be dispatched until the inspection passes.
4. "What this does not cover" is always there. Switch to another language: every card is translated.

Automated checks:

```powershell
Set-Location backend; npx vitest run tests/integration/catalog-assurance.test.ts
Set-Location apps/customer-web; npx vitest run src/pages/AssurancePage.test.tsx
```

### Row 8 — Country / Market Landing (FIXED_AND_VERIFIED)

1. Choose a country in the storefront's EN/₹ menu, scroll to the footer: "Shopping from <country>" opens /markets/<code>.
2. The page shows "Buyers in <country> are quoted in <currency>" and a "Shop as a buyer in <country>" button (or "You are shopping as a buyer in…"). Press it from another country: the header switches market.
3. "What can be sold to <country>" lists blocked categories/products with the reason and those needing documents, or says no restrictions are recorded.
4. In the admin console (http://localhost:5173) → Settings → Market pages: pick the country, type an intro and duties text, enter category slugs like "tools-hardware", leave Publish unticked and save: the storefront page shows no notes. Tick Publish and save: "Notes from <marketplace>" appears. Admin → Audit shows two settings.updated entries for market_profile.
5. Negative: sign in as a catalogue manager (no settings.write) - the panel is read-only; /markets/zz is the not-found page.

Automated checks:

```powershell
Set-Location backend; npx vitest run tests/integration/market-pages.test.ts
Set-Location apps/customer-web; npx vitest run src/pages/MarketPage.test.tsx
Set-Location apps/admin-web; npx vitest run src/pages/settings/MarketPagesPanel.test.tsx
```

### Row 9 — Help / Policies / Legal (NEEDS_HUMAN_VERIFICATION)

1. Open http://localhost:5174/legal (footer: "Help, policies and legal"). You see Get help, and Terms and policies: each published document links to its text; the rest are named under "Not published yet".
2. In the admin console → Settings → Legal documents → New document: the Kind list now offers Seller terms, Privacy policy, Returns policy, Buyer protection policy, Inspection policy and Prohibited products. Create a Returns policy, publish it, reload /legal: it appears with its version and date, and /assurance shows it under "The policies in full".
3. Try to change the published document: it is refused (published documents are immutable). Publish a newer version: the older one stays readable at its own link.
4. Sign-up still asks only for the Terms and Conditions; a policy is never offered as the thing you agree to.
5. Legal sign-off (human): read each published text and confirm it is the approved wording for this business.

Automated checks:

```powershell
Set-Location backend; npx vitest run tests/integration/legal-documents.test.ts
Set-Location apps/customer-web; npx vitest run src/pages/HelpPoliciesPage.test.tsx
```

### Row 10 — Sign Up / Login / MFA (FIXED_AND_VERIFIED)

1. Open http://localhost:5174/register: create an account (the Terms must be ticked); you are asked to confirm your email. /login: signing in with a wrong password shows the same message whether or not the email exists; repeated failures lock the account.
2. /forgot-password: enter an email — the same confirmation appears for a known and an unknown address; the email (real SMTP here) holds a reset link.
3. Signed in, open Account → Profile: "Two-step sign-in" is off. Press "Turn on two-step sign-in", scan the QR code with an authenticator app, save the recovery codes, enter the code: it is on, with "10 recovery codes left".
4. Sign out and back in: after the password you are asked for the six-digit code. A recovery code works once.
5. "Replace recovery codes" asks you to confirm with a code, then shows ten new codes once. "Turn off two-step sign-in" asks for a code and emails the account holder.
6. As a seller owner, the panel explains it stays on and offers no switch-off.

Automated checks:

```powershell
Set-Location backend; npx vitest run tests/integration/auth.test.ts tests/integration/self-registration.test.ts tests/integration/customer-mfa.test.ts tests/integration/rate-limit.test.ts
Set-Location apps/customer-web; npx vitest run src/pages/account/TwoStepSignInPanel.test.tsx src/pages/LoginPage.test.tsx src/pages/RegisterPage.test.tsx
```

### Row 11 — Buyer Profile / KYC (FIXED_AND_VERIFIED)

1. Signed in as a buyer, open http://localhost:5174/account/identity: fill identity details, upload a PDF of the document, Send for review. Details lock. The document number shows only its last characters.
2. In the admin console open the customer → Identity check: Open the file (audited), Accept it, Verify identity. If a colleague decided first, you are told and the panel reloads.
3. Set the document expiry in the past (or wait for it): the check shows Expired and cannot be re-sent until a valid document is entered.
4. As the owner of an approved company open Account → Companies → the company → Team: invite a colleague by email and role, Resend, Withdraw. Sign in as that colleague (same email) and open the emailed link: Accept and join. Change their role or Remove them; removal takes effect at once.
5. A draft company shows "You can invite colleagues once the company is verified."

Automated checks:

```powershell
Set-Location backend; npx vitest run tests/integration/customer-kyc.test.ts tests/integration/buyer-company-team.test.ts tests/integration/buyer-companies.test.ts
Set-Location apps/customer-web; npx vitest run src/pages/account/IdentityPage.test.tsx src/pages/company/CompanyTeamPanel.test.tsx
Set-Location apps/admin-web; npx vitest run src/pages/customer/CustomerKycPanel.test.tsx
```

### Row 12 — Seller Application / KYB (NEEDS_HUMAN_VERIFICATION)

1. In Admin → Seller application, open a submitted seller and confirm every required ownership, registration, export and approval-evidence item is shown without private storage keys or provider configuration details.
2. Configure the production payout provider with real credentials. Complete a test seller's bank verification through the provider, then confirm the application cannot be approved before that result and can proceed only after it is recorded.
3. Record a manual sanctions/restricted-party screening decision with the reviewer, date and evidence. Confirm the UI calls it manual screening. If policy requires automated screening, configure that provider and repeat with a real provider result.
4. Suspend an approved seller and confirm edits to live offer price, tiers and packaging are refused; let a required document expire and confirm the seller moves to ACTION_REQUIRED.

### Row 18 — Quote Comparison (FIXED_AND_VERIFIED)

**How to read this report.** Every checkbox in the Word document has one row here, with a stable ID. A box is ticked in the Word document only when the row says **VERIFIED** or **FIXED_AND_VERIFIED**. That means the feature exists, it is wired into the real application, its permission checks work, its success and failure cases were tested, and the tests passed in this run. Reading code was never enough on its own to tick a box. Everything else stays unticked, whatever the reason.

---

## 1. Executive Summary

The software that exists is well engineered and well tested:

- All 4,415 backend tests pass, along with 1,874 frontend tests across the three apps.
- All three production builds succeed.
- The secret scan is clean, all eight languages are complete, and the checked pages have no accessibility violations.

The checklist, however, describes a larger product than the one in this repository. It describes a **B2B sourcing marketplace** with RFQs, supplier quotes and negotiation, third-party pre-shipment inspection with a dispatch gate, escrow-style protected payment, seller payouts and a dispute console. **None of those subsystems exist in the code.** They are P0 in the checklist, so the release cannot pass its own go-live rule.

Verification also found real defects in features that *do* exist (section 5). **All of them are now fixed, with regression tests** (section 5.1), except one that is really a missing subsystem: nothing stops a seller dispatching without inspection. Two of the fixed defects affected money or legal duties:

- Customer AutoPay spending caps were stored, and promised on screen, but never applied when a card was charged.
- GDPR erasure, and the audit-log retention sweep, wrote to an audit table that the production database grants make read-only, so in production every erasure would have been refused and rolled back.

The remaining defects are listed in section 5.2.

32 of 364 boxes are ticked. That number is low because the bar is high, not because the existing software is poor.

## 2. Overall Recommendation

**NO-GO.** Section 16 of the checklist ("Release condition") and the verification rules both make any of the following a blocker, and all of them are present:

- unresolved P0 functional gaps (130 P0 items failed)
- no mandatory-inspection dispatch gate: a seller can dispatch with no inspection at all
- no seller payouts: statements now exist, off by default, but nothing can pay a seller (decision D13)
- no dispute resolution
- no penetration test, restore drill or counsel approval

A CONDITIONAL GO is not available, because the blockers include missing P0 functionality, not only operational tasks.

This is a recommendation supported by evidence. It is not a management decision. The sign-off tables in the document are untouched.

## 3. Environment and Build Information

- Repository root `C:\Users\HP\Desktop\UBoss-Software`. The package manager is npm, with a separate lockfile per package.
- Applications:
  - `backend/`: Fastify API plus a background worker (`src/worker`).
  - `apps/customer-web/`: storefront, which also contains **Seller Hub** under `/seller/*`.
  - `apps/admin-web/`: operator console.
  - `apps/logistics-web/`: logistics partner portal.
  - There is **no inspection portal**.
- The dev stack is started with `scripts/dev-stack.ps1`. Tests use a separate `uboss_test` database. Both databases were at all 99 migrations (`prisma migrate status`).
- The Word document was backed up before any change to `UBoss_Gloviaa_Mart_Detailed_Screen_Checklist_V2_before_verification.docx`. The two files had identical SHA-256 (`2F74C8EA…CE0C`) before editing.
- DOCX structure found:
  - 172 body paragraphs and 35 tables
  - 364 inline `☐` characters in the Aptos font, with no Word content controls
  - 1 image and 1 footer
  - no comments and no tracked changes

Rules applied:

- **Priority** is exactly what the document states. "No priority" rows (sections 3.3, 5, 7.1, 12, 14, 16, 20, 21) are treated as launch controls and are never deferred.
- A missing P1/P2 capability is **DEFERRED**, because the document's own legend defines P1 as Phase 2 and P2 as Phase 3.
- A **defect** in something that is built is **FAILED** at any priority.
- A P1/P2 item that affects payment integrity (AutoPay) is **FAILED**, not deferred.
- The 12 boxes of the blank per-screen QA template (section 8) are **NOT_APPLICABLE**. They are form fields to be filled in per screen, not requirements.
- The 18 sign-off boxes are **NEEDS_HUMAN_VERIFICATION**.

## 5. P0 Blockers

### 5.1 Defects found and fixed in this verification

Both fixes were chosen by the repository owner when asked, and each has regression tests that fail against the old code.

| Defect | Affects | Fix | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **AutoPay caps ignored at charge time.** `evaluateAutoPay()` had no caller, so a scheduled charge above the customer's own maximum, above their "ask me first" threshold, or while AutoPay was paused, was charged anyway. The AutoPay screen promised otherwise. | SCREEN-085 (now FIXED_AND_VERIFIED), JOURNEY-051, ENH-014, UAT-UI-005, HOME-014 | `backend/src/modules/recurring/occurrence.service.ts` asks `evaluateAutoPay()` before every scheduled off-session charge. **Refuse** (above max or platform ceiling, paused, or limits in another currency): nothing charged, no order created, plan paused, customer emailed the reason. **Ask** (above threshold): order created but not charged, occurrence `ACTION_REQUIRED` with a pay link, so paying it is the approval. Both are audited as `autopay.charge_withheld`. Customers with no account-level setting are unchanged. No new error codes and no state-machine change: the transitions used were already legal. | 5 new tests in `backend/tests/integration/scheduled-orders.test.ts` ("the customer's own auto-pay limits"). Run against the pre-fix code, **3 of the 5 fail** (charge above max, above threshold, while paused), which proves they guard the defect. Full backend suite passes (section 27). |
| **GDPR erasure and audit retention refused by production grants.** Erasure ran `UPDATE audit_logs` inside its transaction, and retention ran `DELETE`. The production grant (correctly) denies both to the app account, so every production erasure would have rolled back. It was invisible because tests run with full grants. | SEC-009, DOD-031 (both still FAILED for other clauses) | New account `uboss_maintenance` (`DATABASE_MAINTENANCE_URL`, required in production). Its grant is column-level: `SELECT`, `DELETE` and `UPDATE (actorEmail, ipAddress, userAgent)` on `audit_logs` only. `auditMaintenancePrisma()` in `backend/src/infra/prisma.ts` uses it. Erasure pseudonymises audit rows just after its commit. A failure fails the erasure loudly, and a rerun completes it. Retention deletes through the same account. `apply-grants.sh` and the generator create and verify the grant, bootstrap and the compat container create the account, and the CI step proves both allowed and forbidden statements. | Proven on real MariaDB 10.4 with throwaway schemas and accounts, dropped afterwards. **SQL:** 11/11 checks. **Generator end to end:** 7/7, including a widened grant reported WRONG and nothing emitted for a missing account. **Real Prisma clients:** the app client was refused with `UPDATE command denied … audit_logs` (the exact production failure), while the maintenance client pseudonymised and deleted, and was refused a rewrite of `action`. New tests: an erasure whose audit step fails does not report success and a rerun finishes it (`data-subject-rights.test.ts`); production refuses a missing or shared maintenance URL (`production-config.test.ts`). |

**Second round (29 Sep 2026, at the owner's request): the remaining open defects.**

| Defect | Affects | Fix | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Home cards led to switched-off features.** The Autopay and ERP cards linked through even though both features are off by default. | HOME-014, HOME-016, DYNAMIC-007 | `/config` publishes `customerAutopay` and `customerErp`. A switched-off card shows a note, translated into 8 languages, instead of linking. An older config without the flags counts as off. | `greeting.test.tsx`: 36 pass, including 3 new. `public-config.test.ts`: 9 pass, including a new check that the flags follow the settings (AutoPay needs both of its flags). |
| **Dead-letter queues led to Not Found.** The dashboard counted dead jobs and failed emails, but linked to routes that did not exist, and there was no screen for either. | SCREEN-065, SCREEN-077, JOURNEY-060, JOURNEY-065, DOD-050 | New admin screens `/operations/dead-jobs` and `/operations/failed-notifications`, with 4 endpoints (read needs `settings.read`, retry needs `settings.write`). They show no job payload or email body, and the recipient is masked. A retry is exactly one more attempt: conditional on the dead state, it returns 409 on a second press. Retrying an email also re-arms its unique delivery job, without which a manual reset silently does nothing. An erased recipient is refused. Every retry is audited. | `dead-letter.test.ts`: 10 new, covering masking, no payload, exactly once, 403 without write, 401 for a customer or anonymous user, the re-arm, the erased case and the dashboard hrefs. `DeadLetterPage.test.tsx`: 8 new. Admin strings added in 8 languages. |
| **No portal user could ever mark a shipment delivered.** DELIVERED needs a proof of delivery for every shipment, and the portal had no way to capture one. | SCREEN-057, JOURNEY-047 | A "Complete the delivery" dialog that asks for what the shipment's SLA policy requires. The shipment detail now returns `podRequirements`. Photos and signatures are uploaded first, then the capture is sent under one idempotency key per opening; recording it moves the shipment to DELIVERED. | `ProofOfDeliveryDialog.test.tsx`: 6 new. `logistics-pod.test.ts`: 7 new, which previously had no test at all. |
| **A repeat capture after a successful one was refused**: "no longer assigned to your company". This was found by the new test. | SCREEN-057 | `pod.service.ts` now checks for a duplicate first (still scoped to the carrier) and requires write access only for a new capture. | Observed failing before the fix and passing after, together with the acceptance, tenant-isolation and state-machine suites (43 pass). |
| **Seller statements were never generated**, so the Seller Hub Payments page was always empty. | SCREEN-043, JOURNEY-034 | The operator chose "operator-set, off by default". There are three settings: `FEATURE_SELLER_SETTLEMENT_STATEMENTS`, `SELLER_SETTLEMENT_PERIOD` and `SELLER_SETTLEMENT_PAYABLE_AFTER_DAYS`, which has **no default** (start-up refuses without it). A daily job closes each period into one numbered statement per seller and currency, copying figures from each order's settlement record. Refunds are carried as the difference only, and the header identity is checked before writing. New unique index `uq_seller_settlement_period_currency` (migration `20261013090000`) plus row locks make it idempotent. The page shows the period in UTC as the days it covers, and labels each line in the reader's language. It **moves no money**. | `seller-settlement-statements.test.ts`: 6 new, covering off by default, the return window, per-currency statements, the identity, idempotent and concurrent closes, refund difference and give-back across four months, and a payout still refused. `SellerPaymentsPage.test.tsx`: 2 new. `production-config.test.ts`: 2 new. `job-handlers.test.ts` passes (the new job has a handler). |

Each row above still has other unmet clauses (listed in section 7), so fixing the defect ticked no additional box.

**Third round (29 Sep 2026, at the owner's request): customer AutoPay and the customer's own ERP are on by default.**

- **Defaults:** `FEATURE_CUSTOMER_AUTOPAY`, `FEATURE_SUBSCRIPTION_AUTOPAY` and `FEATURE_CUSTOMER_ERP` now default to `true`. AutoPay needs card saving, so both of its flags had to change together.
- **AutoPay is offered only once Stripe is connected.** The owner chose this over keeping a start-up refusal. The check covers Settings > Payments or the env keys: `isCustomerAutoPayAvailable` / `isCardEnrolmentAvailable` in `payments/payment.service.ts`, built on `availableGateways`, which mirrors what a real payment would use.
  - Until then, the AutoPay routes and card enrolment refuse with `FEATURE_DISABLED`.
  - `/account/autopay` reports `available: false`.
  - The home card explains that AutoPay isn't offered.
- **The two production start-up refusals were removed.** They fired whenever no Stripe secret key was set in the environment, and they ignored a gateway connected in Settings. So the store now starts without Stripe, and nobody is asked to consent to charges that can't be made. The rule "customer AutoPay needs card saving" stays.
- **Evidence:** `public-config.test.ts` gained "offers Autopay only once Stripe is connected, although the flags are on by default". The 11 suites covering AutoPay, card saving, payments, Stripe Checkout, payment links, schedules, production config and the buyer ERP pass: **384 tests**. The full backend suite was then re-run (see section 27).
- **Checklist effect:** no status changed. The affected rows' evidence now states the new defaults.

**Found while fixing, and still open:**

- A delivery policy that requires a delivery code can never be completed, because this installation never issues one (`issueDeliveryOtp` has no caller). The dialog says so and disables the button.
- A driver cannot open the shipment page: the driver role lacks `logistics.shipment.read`, so in practice owners and admins record deliveries.

### 5.2 Defects still open in built features

| ID | Priority | Defect |
| -- | -- | -- |
| LIVE-008 | None stated | The gate does not exist, so today a seller CAN dispatch without inspection (seller-state.ts SHIPPED transitions; shipment PICKED_UP/IN_TRANSIT by SELLER). |

### 5.3 Missing P0 subsystems (not built)

- **Third-party inspection and dispatch gate**: FLOW-*, INSPECT-*, SCREEN-045…055, 070, 094, 095, JOURNEY-035…045 and LIVE-007…009.
  - Size estimate: 12–15 tables, a new agency/inspector actor with its own portal, about 16 screens, and guards on three existing state machines.
- **B2B RFQ, supplier match, quotes, quote comparison, negotiation, PO/contract review**: SCREEN-016…021, 038, 090 and JOURNEY-013…020.
- **Dispute / claim and dispute console**: SCREEN-024, 064 and JOURNEY-058.
- **Buyer return / refund request screen**: SCREEN-030.
- **Single transaction ledger, protected collection (escrow), seller payouts**: SCREEN-058…060 and JOURNEY-050, 052, 053. These depend on open management decision D13 (merchant of record or facilitator).

## 8–24. Results by Area

Each area lists its counts and what is ticked. The detailed rows are in section 7. Where one piece of evidence supports several items, the rows say so ("see SCREEN-027"), but each item was judged on its own requirement text.


### Screen inventory (SCREEN-001 … 097)

97 checkboxes — Verified 4 · Fixed and verified 3 · Failed 72 · Needs human verification 1 · Deferred 17.

Of the 97 screens, the ones that exist and pass every check are listed below. The master list assumes a B2B sourcing product with RFQs, quotes, third-party inspection, escrow and disputes; those screens do not exist in this codebase (see the P0 blockers).

Checked: SCREEN-027, SCREEN-028, SCREEN-032, SCREEN-073, SCREEN-079, SCREEN-080, SCREEN-085.


### Standard Definition of Done (DOD-001 … 050)

50 checkboxes — Verified 14 · Fixed and verified 2 · Failed 30 · Blocked 2 · Needs human verification 2.

A DoD control is ticked only where it is a platform-wide mechanism that is enforced in one place and covered by a passing test. Controls that depend on each screen being checked, on people, or on screens that do not exist are not ticked.

Checked: DOD-006, DOD-009, DOD-010, DOD-014, DOD-015, DOD-020, DOD-024, DOD-025, DOD-027, DOD-029, DOD-033, DOD-034, DOD-036, DOD-042, DOD-045, DOD-047.


### Authentication and authorization

10 checkboxes — Verified 3 · Failed 6 · Deferred 1.

Session, lockout, token rotation, audience separation and tenant isolation are strong and tested. MFA exists for staff and logistics owners/admins only; sellers and buyers have none.

Checked: DOD-014, DOD-015, DOD-025.


### Buyer and seller onboarding

6 checkboxes — Failed 6.

Buyer company KYB is well built and tested. Seller KYB lacks bank validation, beneficial owners, export data and sanctions screening; factory verification does not exist.

Checked: none.


### B2B RFQ and negotiation

22 checkboxes — Failed 20 · Deferred 2.

There is no RFQ, quote, supplier-invitation, sample or PO-review subsystem. The bulk preorder is a single-product, single-seller analogue with versioned offers and a terms hash.

Checked: none.


### B2C commerce

14 checkboxes — Verified 2 · Fixed and verified 1 · Failed 8 · Deferred 3.

Cart, checkout idempotency and the payment result are solid. Buyer returns have no screen; duties are never shown; there is no consent step at checkout.

Checked: SCREEN-027, SCREEN-028, SCREEN-032.


### Preorders

5 checkboxes — Failed 4 · Deferred 1.

The DOCX has no dedicated preorder checkboxes. The preorder module is well tested (preorders*.test.ts, preorder-chat*.test.ts) and is cited as the nearest analogue for negotiation and messaging items.

Checked: none.


### Seller Hub

24 checkboxes — Failed 20 · Deferred 4.

Listings, documents, packing lists and the one-hour Hub lock are strong. Seller settlement statements are never generated and there is no payout provider.

Checked: none.


### Logistics and tracking

7 checkboxes — Failed 4 · Deferred 3.

L1–L4 levels, carrier quotes/purchase, signed carrier webhooks and tenant isolation are tested. No proof-of-delivery capture screen exists; real carrier contracts are untested.

Checked: none.


### Inspection and dispatch gate

44 checkboxes — Failed 38 · Deferred 6.

Nothing in this section exists. The most serious consequence: a seller can mark goods shipped with no inspection at all, so "dispatch before PASS" is not blocked — it is simply unguarded.

Checked: none.


### Payment, refund, invoice and settlement

14 checkboxes — Verified 2 · Fixed and verified 1 · Failed 8 · Deferred 3.

Stripe test mode works end to end at the API; amount/currency tampering, duplicate and delayed webhooks, and cross-customer access are all refused in tests. Seller payouts, escrow, a single ledger and dispute handling do not exist, and AutoPay caps are not enforced at charge time.

Checked: SCREEN-085, SEC-006, UAT-UI-014.


### ERP and integrations

11 checkboxes — Verified 1 · Fixed and verified 1 · Failed 3 · Blocked 2 · Deferred 4.

Buyer ERP and operator ERP pipelines have idempotency and retry, tested. Tally has never met a real installation; no live carrier or payout credentials exist. Nothing shows "Connected" without a passing test in the buyer ERP flow (activation requires a passed test).

Checked: DOD-033, DOD-034.


### Security, privacy and trust

17 checkboxes — Verified 4 · Failed 8 · Blocked 2 · Needs human verification 3.

Automated controls are strong. Found a real defect: GDPR erasure and audit retention write to audit_logs, which the production database grants forbid. External pen test, DR drill and counsel review have not happened.

Checked: DOD-025, DOD-027, DOD-029, SEC-006.


### Accessibility and responsive

7 checkboxes — Verified 2 · Failed 5.

Browser sweep: 14 storefront pages at 320/375/768/1024/1440 in light and dark — no page-level sideways scroll, zero axe WCAG 2.0/2.1 A+AA violations, logical focus order with visible focus. Gaps: at 320 px the brand shrinks to "G."; several controls are under 24×24 px (WCAG 2.2); admin, Seller Hub and logistics screens were not swept; no screen-reader or Firefox/WebKit run.

Checked: SCREEN-080, HOME-002.


### Localization and currency

4 checkboxes — Verified 2 · Failed 2.

Eight languages at full coverage in all three apps (npm run check:i18n). Prices are real per-currency price lists, not silent conversions. Changing country does not change serviceability.

Checked: SCREEN-079, DOD-006.


### UAT scenarios

15 checkboxes — Verified 2 · Fixed and verified 1 · Failed 12.

Section 11's UAT-01…UAT-15 table has no checkboxes; it is covered through the items above. Of the 15 checkbox scenarios in section 21, the ticked ones were run in a real browser or against Stripe test mode.

Checked: UAT-UI-003, UAT-UI-014, UAT-UI-015.


### Performance and reliability

7 checkboxes — Verified 1 · Fixed and verified 1 · Failed 3 · Blocked 1 · Needs human verification 1.

No load test, no network-fault test and no low-bandwidth detection exist. Reliability logic (retries, dead letters, idempotency) is tested in code.

Checked: DOD-042, UAT-UI-015.

## 29. Rollback and Recovery Readiness

- Backup, restore and binlog-shipping scripts exist: `deploy/scripts/backup.sh`, `ship-binlogs.sh`, `scripts/db/verify-restore.ps1`, and `docs/DATABASE-RECOVERY.md`.
- A restore of a local development dump was tested on 2026-09-16.
- A production backup has **never** been restored. There is no DR environment, and no point-in-time recovery has been rehearsed (SEC-010, LIVE-015).
- Application rollback is `deploy/scripts/release.sh` with the previous release. It has not been exercised, because no production host exists.
- For this verification, rollback is simple:
  - Restore the DOCX from the backup file, and delete `Checklist.md` and `verification-evidence/`.
  - The two code fixes are uncommitted working-tree changes. `git diff` shows them, and `git checkout -- <files>` reverts them.
  - The first-round fixes add no migration and change no data. A deployment that had already created `uboss_maintenance` can simply keep the unused account.
  - The second round adds one migration: a unique index on `seller_settlements`. It changes no data. Reverting it is `DROP INDEX uq_seller_settlement_period_currency ON seller_settlements`, then marking the migration rolled back (`prisma migrate resolve --rolled-back 20261013090000_seller_settlement_statements`).
  - Statements are off by default, so a deployment that never switches them on writes no statement rows.

They match exactly: SCREEN-027, SCREEN-028, SCREEN-032, SCREEN-073, SCREEN-079, SCREEN-080, SCREEN-085, DOD-006, DOD-009, DOD-010, DOD-014, DOD-015, DOD-020, DOD-024, DOD-025, DOD-027, DOD-029, DOD-033, DOD-034, DOD-036, DOD-042, DOD-045, DOD-047, SEC-006, HOME-002, HOME-004, HOME-008, HOME-012, HOME-017, UAT-UI-003, UAT-UI-014, UAT-UI-015.

- After the last edit, the updated DOCX and a fresh render of the untouched backup were both rendered to PDF with Microsoft Word 16, in the same session. Every page was turned into an image and compared pixel by pixel.
  - Why a fresh render was needed: a control run showed that Word renders the *unchanged* backup slightly differently between sessions (text anti-aliasing, most likely a cloud-font refresh), so a render from earlier in the day could not serve as the baseline.
  - Both documents have **29 pages**.
  - **22 pages are pixel-identical** to the original.
  - On pages 4, 5, 6 (three ticks, including SCREEN-085), 17, 21 and 28, the only change is a box-sized region (about 10×10 px) at each ticked box.

  - Header, footer, image, table widths and both sign-off tables are unchanged.
- Each ticked box was checked visually on its intended row.

## 31. Final GO / CONDITIONAL GO / NO-GO Recommendation

**NO-GO**, for the reasons in sections 2 and 5.

The shortest honest path to a CONDITIONAL GO:

1. Decide the launch scope. Either build the inspection gate, RFQ and disputes, or formally re-baseline the checklist to the product that exists. Re-baselining is a Product Owner decision, not something this report can do.
2. Act on the two issues found while fixing (section 5.1): delivery codes are never issued, and drivers cannot open the shipment page. Every other defect found in built features was fixed in this verification.
3. Decide D13 and integrate a payout provider.
4. Complete the external items in section 26.
